- [2026-07-20 14:30]: iCloud Sync — Diagnostics Surface (hardening commit 1/7)
  - *Details*: First commit of the iCloud-sync hardening pass. Investigation into "habits synced to the Mac but macro Goals did not" established that the goals ARE imported and marked dirty on the iPhone, and that the failure is a push that dies partway through the first mass upload. Three causes were ruled out by evidence (import validation drop, E2E key mismatch, change-token livelock — all refuted because habits decrypted and applied on the Mac). The remaining cause is push ordering plus the total absence of retry: `markAllDirty()` enqueues in `syncedTables` order (profiles → goals/habits → goal_logs → long_term_goals → …) and `dirtyEntries()` has no ORDER BY, so habits ride in batch 1 while macro goals sit thousands of records deep; no CloudKit bridge honours `retryAfterSeconds` and no CKOperation sets `qualityOfService`.
    This commit adds NO behaviour change — it makes the failure visible, because `last_full_sync_at` was stamped even when every record failed and `sync_state.last_error` was written by the engine and read by no code in either app.
    An audit of the sync layer (48 agents, adversarially verified) produced 35 confirmed findings; the remaining 6 commits of this pass address them. Two are data-integrity issues, logged in TO_SIMO_DO.md.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **New** `packages/evolve_sync/lib/src/sync_diagnostics.dart` — `SyncDiagnostics` value type: `localRowsByTable`, `pendingByTable`, `pendingDeletesByTable`, `errorsByReason`, `parkedByReason`, `hasChangeToken`, `lastFullSyncAt`; derived `totalPending`/`totalErrors`/`totalParked`/`isFullySynced`; `toReport()` renders a copyable fixed-width per-table table. Exported from `evolve_sync.dart`.
    - **New** `SyncLocalStore.diagnostics()` — read-only, no transaction, safe to call mid-sync. Errors are split on `dirty` (will-retry vs parked) rather than on `quarantineStamp`, because `quarantineRecord`'s ON CONFLICT branch updates only `last_error` and leaves an existing row's stamp intact, which a stamp-based test would misclassify.
    - **Interface**: `PrivateSyncService.diagnostics()` → `Future<SyncDiagnostics?>` (null = no store to inspect). Implemented in `CloudKitPrivateSyncService` (off the `_runExclusive` lock — a pure read that must work while a long push is running; swallows errors so a locked/corrupt DB does not take the screen down) and `NoOpPrivateSyncService`. Six test fakes updated.
    - **Mobile UI** `mobile/lib/ui/screens/icloud_sync_screen.dart`: "Sync details" row (hidden when diagnostics is null) with a summary line — failures outrank the pending count — opening an `showEvolveSheet` with the monospace report and a copy-to-clipboard action.
    - **Desktop UI** `desktop/lib/features/settings/presentation/settings_page.dart`: same row + `EvolveAlertDialog` with the report and copy action.
    - **Fonts**: platform monospace (`Menlo`, fallback `Courier New`/`monospace`) rather than `GoogleFonts.robotoMono` — GoogleFonts fetches at runtime and this screen must render offline.
    - **i18n**: 7 new `icloudSync.*` keys on mobile and 6 on desktop across en/it/es/de/ar; `dart run slang` re-run for both apps.
    - **Tests**: new `packages/evolve_sync/test/sync_diagnostics_test.dart` (7 tests, incl. one reproducing the exact reported shape — early tables acknowledged, `long_term_goals` left pending); 5 new mobile widget tests; 2 new desktop widget tests. Verified: evolve_sync 106/106, mobile 404/404, desktop 445 passing with the SAME 6 failures present on a clean tree (`desktop_supabase_config_security_test`, `habits_page_keyboard_test`, `widget_test` — pre-existing, confirmed by stashing).
  - *Current Status*: Commit 1/7 complete. **Immediate next step**: user runs "Sync details" on both iPhone and Mac Mini and reports the per-table counts; that confirms the push-stall diagnosis before commit 2 (stop reporting success on failure) and commit 3 (FK-safe `applyDelete`).

- [2026-07-20 21:50]: iCloud Sync — Key-Mint Guard + Undecryptable Quarantine (hardening commit 2/7)
  - *Details*: Real-device diagnostics REFUTED the earlier push-stall hypothesis and identified the true root cause. iPhone reported `pending: 0` on every table with 6234 local rows; Mac reported 0 rows everywhere; both logged `InvalidCipherTextException` on pull. The decisive evidence was the asymmetry: the only records the iPhone could not decrypt were `profiles`×1 and `goal_category_settings`×1 — exactly the two rows the Mac had created — while the Mac could decrypt none of the iPhone's. **Two divergent E2E keys.**
    Mechanism: `SyncKeyStore.getOrCreateKeyReporting()` mints a key whenever `readKey()` returns null, and `readKey()` cannot distinguish "no key exists" from "the key has not yet propagated through the iCloud Keychain". The Mac enabled during that window, minted a second key, and every record in the zone became permanently unreadable by every device. `migrating_sync_secret_store.dart:46` had predicted this exact failure; the mitigation was iOS-only.
    Secondary defect: a decrypt failure was classified as TRANSIENT, so it held the change token and rewound it every sync — the iPhone's `change token: none`, re-downloading and discarding 6238 records indefinitely.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **`CloudKitBridge.zoneHasRecords()`** (new): cheap, side-effect-free probe — `fetchAllChanges = false`, `desiredKeys = []`, returned token DISCARDED so probing can never advance a device past unapplied changes. Native impl added to BOTH `mobile/ios/Runner/AppDelegate.swift` and `desktop/macos/Runner/AppDelegate.swift` (dispatch case + `zoneHasRecords`). `zoneNotFound`/`userDeletedZone` ⇒ false; any other error ⇒ **fails closed** (`true`, defer) in both Swift and `cloudkit_bridge_method_channel.dart`, because answering "empty" on an inconclusive probe is the branch that mints.
    - **The guard** (`sync_engine.dart` `enable()`): if `readKey() == null && await bridge.zoneHasRecords()` ⇒ return `SyncResult(keyPending: true)` and touch NOTHING — no key write, no owner publish, no `markAllDirty`. New `SyncResult.keyPending`; `ran` now excludes it.
    - **Undecryptable quarantine**: `InvalidCipherTextException` caught in `_applyRemote` ⇒ `quarantineRecord(reason: SyncLocalStore.undecryptableReason)` + new `_ApplyOutcome.undecryptable`, which advances the token instead of holding it. Counted into `SyncResult.undecryptable` and logged once per pull with the total.
    - **Key-rotation recovery (schema v5)**: advancing the token would otherwise LOSE those records permanently (CloudKit never re-delivers past an advanced token). Added `sync_meta.key_fingerprint` (SHA-256 of the key truncated to 64 bits, one-way, never transmitted — `SyncCrypto.keyFingerprint`). `syncNow` compares it; on change it calls `clearUndecryptableParks()` and drops the change token, forcing ONE full re-fetch that re-applies everything now readable. `_upgradeToV5` is idempotent AND guards against a missing `sync_meta` (a migration must not assume its predecessor's side effects).
    - **Behaviour change, deliberate**: `sync_engine_test.dart`'s "a record that FAILS to apply holds the change token" no longer holds the token for a decrypt failure. Recovery is preserved via the fingerprint re-fetch rather than the token hold; the assertion was updated with the rationale. Holding recovers the same records but livelocks forever when the key never arrives — which is the production state observed.
    - **Tests**: new `test/sync_key_split_test.dart` (8 tests) covering the guard (defer / no key written / nothing touched / genuinely-first still mints), the quarantine (token advances, second sync does not re-report), and full recovery when the correct key arrives later. Two `CloudKitBridge` wrappers in `sync_convergence_test.dart` gained `zoneHasRecords` delegation. Verified: evolve_sync **114/114**, mobile **404/404**, desktop **451/451** (with dart-defines).
  - *Current Status*: Commit 2/7 complete. The guard PREVENTS a future key split; it does NOT repair the existing one (both devices already hold keys, so the guard never fires). **Immediate next step**: commit 3 — the "Reset sync from this device" recovery action plus UI surfacing of `keyPending`/`undecryptable`, which is what actually recovers the user's data.

- [2026-07-20 23:10]: iCloud Sync — Reset Recovery + Key-Split UI (hardening commit 3/7)
  - *Details*: The repair path for the two-key split identified in commit 2, plus the UI that makes both the split and the deferred-enable state visible. Commit 2 prevents a future split but cannot fix an existing one (the guard only fires when a device has NO key, and both of the user's devices hold one — just different ones). This commit adds the deliberate, user-confirmed escape hatch, per the agreed Q4(a)+(b) / Q6(a) policy: never resolve a key conflict automatically, always give the user an explicit way through.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **`PrivateSyncService.resetSyncFromThisDevice()`** (new): wipe the zone → `keys.deleteAll()` (so the iCloud Keychain propagates the removal and the rival device stops using its key) → `store.resetSyncState()` → re-enable as first device. Local user data untouched. Step 3 is load-bearing: after a normal enable nothing is dirty, so without clearing `sync_state`/token/fingerprint the re-enable would push ZERO records and the reset would produce an empty zone — covered by a dedicated regression test.
    - **`SyncLocalStore.resetSyncState()`** (new): drops every `sync_state` row plus `server_change_token`, `key_fingerprint` and `last_full_sync_at`. All of it describes a zone that no longer exists.
    - **`enable({bool force})`** through engine → service → both UIs. `force` skips the populated-zone guard and is reachable ONLY from a confirmed destructive dialog; documented as never-from-an-automatic-path. It exists so a device whose iCloud Keychain is disabled (and which therefore will never receive the shared key) is not permanently deferred — the lockout bug the commit-2 guard would otherwise have introduced.
    - **`PrivateSyncStatus.keyPending` / `.undecryptableCount`** (new). `_status()` falls back to reading the parked count from the store when the current op reports 0, so a split stays visible on a screen opened days later rather than only in the sync that discovered it.
    - **UI (both apps)**: `_statusLabel` now returns the key-split copy instead of "Up to date" whenever `undecryptableCount > 0` — the specific lie that hid this failure. Mobile gets a destructive warning card with an inline "Reset sync from this device"; desktop gets an equivalent `_ActionRow`. Both wire the deferred-enable path to a "Start fresh from this device" confirmation.
    - **i18n**: 10 new `icloudSync.*` keys × 5 locales × 2 apps; `dart run slang` re-run for both.
    - **Tests**: 4 new in `cloudkit_private_sync_service_test.dart` (fresh key minted, rival records gone, re-uploads despite everything being marked synced, local data untouched, split visible long after the sync that found it); 5 new mobile widget tests (never says "Up to date", reset needs confirmation, confirmation runs it, deferred enable offers the override, override needs its own confirmation). Five test fakes updated for the widened interface. Verified: evolve_sync **118/118**, mobile **409/409**, desktop **451/451**.
  - *Current Status*: Commits 1–3 complete; the recovery path is implemented and unit-tested but has NEVER run against real CloudKit. **Immediate next step**: user builds to iPhone + Mac and performs the documented recovery (TO_SIMO_DO.md). Remaining: honest success reporting (old commit 2), FK-safe `applyDelete` (old commit 3, data-loss path still live), mobile launch/periodic sync, retry/backoff + QoS, `quarantineRecord` ON CONFLICT stamp fix.

- [2026-07-20 23:40]: iCloud Sync — On-Device Recovery VERIFIED + reset log fix
  - *Details*: The commit-1..3 chain was executed on real hardware (iPhone + Mac Mini). The reset ran from the iPhone, both devices now report "Everything uploaded", and a goal created on the iPhone appeared on macOS immediately — bidirectional sync confirmed working end to end.
    Log analysis of the post-recovery iOS export confirms the remaining errors are HISTORICAL, not live: the two `InvalidCipherTextException` quarantines are stamped 22:26:09, ~25s BEFORE the reset at 22:26:34, and name the same two record UUIDs (`goal_category_settings:1184a172…`, `profiles:e4353ba9…`) seen in the original broken logs — i.e. the Mac's old key-split records. Every sync after 22:27:22 shows zero decrypt errors: `skipped: 6235` (one-time full re-fetch, expected — `resetSyncState()` nulls the change token), then `{pushed: 0, applied: 0, skipped: 0}`, then `{pushed: 1, …}` for the newly created goal. The macOS errors clearing after an app restart are the same stale-state artefact.
    This also validates commit 2 on real hardware: the undecryptable records were quarantined rather than held, the token advanced, and the count surfaced with an accurate message — the exact behaviour that replaced the permanent livelock.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **Bug found and fixed in `resetSyncFromThisDevice()` logging**: it logged `res.appliedChanges` under the key `pushed`. `PrivateSyncStatus` carries only a PULL count (0 by definition after a zone wipe) and no push count, so a successful ~6000-record upload logged as `pushed: 0` — a false signal of exactly the kind this hardening pass exists to eliminate. Now logs `localRows` / `stillPending` / `errors` read from `store.diagnostics()`, which describe what the reset actually achieved. evolve_sync **118/118** after the change.
    - Known accepted cost: the first sync after a reset re-downloads the whole zone the device just uploaded (`skipped: 6235`), because the reset nulls the change token and CloudKit returns no token from a push. One-time, correct, not worth optimising.
  - *Current Status*: Recovery verified on device. **Immediate next step**: confirm the per-table backfill actually landed on the Mac (compare Sync details counts on both devices — "Everything uploaded" only proves `pending == 0`, not that the Mac received 3485 goals). Then the FK-safe `applyDelete` data-loss fix, which remains live.

- [2026-07-21 00:40]: iCloud Sync — FK-safe applyDelete + tombstone ordering + owner-scoped diagnostics (hardening commit 4/7)
  - *Details*: A 33-agent adversarial investigation into the observed `profiles` 3/2 and `goal_category_settings` 3/2 divergence returned 21 confirmed findings (8 refuted). Root cause of the duplicate identities: **inverted bootstrap ordering**. `_open()` calls `_ensureProfile` (mobile private_local_database.dart:199-205) / `seedProfile` (desktop desktop_private_db.dart:1421-1422), which materializes a `profiles` + `goal_category_settings` PAIR keyed on the CURRENT owner id, and only afterwards runs `_reconcileOrphanedOwner`, which discovers that id owns zero data rows and adopts the id that does. The row just created is never removed, and its INSERT trigger marks it dirty so it replicates. `goal_category_settings.user_id` is UNIQUE, so its count equals the number of distinct owner ids ever seeded — which is why profiles and goal_category_settings move in lockstep on both devices (3/3, 2/2). That 1:1 lockstep is the fingerprint of this one function.
    The verifier corrected the proposed trigger: no Keychain failure is required (a blanket device-local Keychain miss fails closed before the DB even opens). The real trigger is a **non-atomic second-device re-key**: `sync_engine.dart:196` `reKeyOwner` commits the DB onto `canonical`, but `ownerWriter(canonical)` only persists that id to the Keychain afterwards, back in `cloudkit_private_sync_service.dart:185-189`. Any interruption between the two leaves the DB re-keyed while `ownerId()` still returns the old id — next open seeds an orphan pair.
    **Correction to an earlier claim in this log**: the "content tables match exactly" evidence was an UNSCOPED `COUNT(*)`. It proves the two devices converged on the same row SET; it does NOT prove those rows belong to the active identity. An identity split produces identical matching counts on every device.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **STAGE 1 — `SyncLocalStore.applyDelete` is now FK-safe** (sync_local_store.dart:380). It was the ONLY mutating apply path running under the connection-wide `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` set in `PrivateDbSchema.onConfigure`. `profiles` is the `ON DELETE CASCADE` parent of every synced table, so one pulled `profiles` tombstone deleted the entire database — and each cascaded row fired its own delete trigger, marking the wipe dirty so it PROPAGATED to every other device. Now bracketed with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF/ON` outside the transaction, mirroring `applyUpsert`.
    - **STAGE 2 — tombstones apply last** (sync_engine.dart, new `_deletePriorityBase = 100`). `_applyPriority` sorted by table only, putting a `profiles` tombstone at priority 0 — ahead of the child upserts that re-point rows off the dying identity. Deletions now sort after every upsert, so the apply is correct on its own terms instead of leaving the FK guard as the single line of defence.
    - **Owner-scoped diagnostics**: `SyncDiagnostics` gains `ownedRowsByTable`, `distinctOwnerCount` and `orphanedRows`; `SyncLocalStore.diagnostics({owner})` counts per identity (`profiles.id`, `user_id` elsewhere) and the service passes the active owner. `toReport()` now prints a `mine` column, an `owners:` line and a `<-- N HIDDEN` marker. This makes "is any data stranded under a dead identity?" answerable in-app instead of requiring hand-decryption of the database.
    - **STAGES 3-4 DELIBERATELY NOT SHIPPED**: the identity reconcile (delete orphan profiles after re-pointing children) must not ship until stage 1 is running on BOTH devices, or a correct local reconcile still cascades on the peer. Per the workflow's explicit staging recommendation.
    - **Tests**: new `test/apply_delete_cascade_test.dart` (5 tests) — a `profiles` tombstone against 3,487 macro goals leaves all 3,487 intact, no cascaded tombstones are queued for upload, a category tombstone does not strip `category_id`, FK enforcement is restored afterwards, and a mixed parent-tombstone/child-upsert batch keeps its children. Verified: evolve_sync **123/123**, mobile **409/409**, desktop **451/451**.
  - *Current Status*: Commits 1-4 complete. **Immediate next step**: user rebuilds BOTH devices (stage 1 must be present on both before any dedupe), then sends the new Sync details — the `mine`/`HIDDEN` columns settle whether any of the 3,487 goals are stranded. Then stages 3-4 (identity reconcile), then desktop settings hydration (`_loadProfilePreferences` is Supabase-only, confirmed on-device as the cause of the orange/yellow accent + language mismatch).

- [2026-07-21 08:15]: iCloud Sync — Orphan identity reap + seed ordering + re-key atomicity (hardening commit 5/7)
  - *Details*: On-device owner-scoped diagnostics settled the risk question decisively: EVERY row of real user data on both devices is owned by the active identity (170/170 moods, 2561/2561 logs, 20/20 habits, 3487/3487 macro goals, 7/7 categories). The only hidden rows are the abandoned identity shells themselves — iPhone `owners: 2` (2 hidden), Mac `owners: 3` (4 hidden), entirely `profiles` + `goal_category_settings` pairs owning nothing. The reconcile is therefore a REAP, not a migration: no user data has to move, which removes the scenario that could have destroyed 3,487 goals.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **`SyncLocalStore.reapOrphanIdentities(canonical)`** (new): deletes `profiles` rows that are neither canonical nor own any row in `_identityDataTables` (goals, goal_logs, daily_moods, long_term_goals, macro_goal_categories — `profiles`/`goal_category_settings` excluded as identity scaffolding), together with their `goal_category_settings` shell. Returns the reaped ids.
      **LOCAL ONLY, per the agreed Q7(a).** Each deleted record's `sync_state` row is snapshotted before the delete and restored verbatim afterwards: this erases the tombstones the delete triggers wrote (so nothing propagates) and preserves the original `updated_at` (so the engine's LWW treats the lingering cloud copy as not-newer and a full re-fetch cannot resurrect it). Chosen over propagating tombstones because the re-key writes the DB before the Keychain, so a device can transiently hold a stale active owner — letting one device's identity verdict delete rows on another risks a device deleting its own live profile and re-seeding in a loop. Accepted cost: the orphan record lingers in CloudKit (a few hundred bytes).
      Deletes run with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` and remove the child explicitly rather than relying on CASCADE, so exactly the intended rows go and a constraint added later cannot drag more along.
      **The precondition is load-bearing** and guards two hazards: an orphan that owns data needs a migration and a human decision, and a device whose active owner is transiently stale would otherwise reap the real canonical identity — which owns everything, so it is always skipped. Both are covered by dedicated tests.
      Called from `CloudKitPrivateSyncService._syncNow` AFTER the engine sync, so a profile row still arriving from another device has already landed and cannot be mistaken for an orphan.
    - **Seed ordering fixed (the root cause)**: `_reconcileOrphanedOwner` now runs BEFORE `_ensureProfile` (mobile private_local_database.dart) / `seedProfile` (desktop desktop_private_db.dart). Seeding first minted a full identity for a stale owner id and the self-heal then adopted a different one, stranding the pair permanently and replicating it via its INSERT trigger.
    - **Re-key atomicity fixed**: `SyncEngine.enable` takes an optional `adoptOwner` callback invoked BEFORE `store.reKeyOwner`, wired to `ownerWriter`. Previously the Keychain write happened after `enable()` returned, so an interruption left the database re-keyed while `ownerId()` still returned the old id — the next open then seeded an orphan for it. The inverted order fails the other way (id adopted, rows not yet moved), which the orphaned-owner self-heal already detects and repairs. The existing post-hoc write is kept as a retry.
    - **Tests**: new `test/orphan_identity_reap_test.dart` (7 tests) — reaps shells while leaving 3,487 goals untouched, REFUSES an identity owning data, refuses to reap the real identity when handed a stale active owner, queues no tombstone, preserves `sync_state` so the cloud copy cannot resurrect the row, idempotent, and diagnostics report `owners: 1` / `orphanedRows: 0` afterwards. Verified: evolve_sync **130/130**, mobile **409/409**, desktop **451/451**.
  - *Current Status*: Commits 1-5 complete. **Immediate next step**: rebuild both devices; the reap runs automatically on the next sync and Sync details should report `owners: 1` with no HIDDEN markers on both. Remaining: desktop settings hydration (`_loadProfilePreferences` is Supabase-only — confirmed on-device cause of the orange/yellow accent + language mismatch), honest success reporting, mobile launch/periodic sync, retry/backoff + QoS, `quarantineRecord` ON CONFLICT stamp.

- [2026-07-21 10:30]: Desktop (macOS) — settings read-back, apply, and post-pull re-hydration
  - *Details*: The Mac wrote settings into the synced row and read back NONE of them. `_loadProfilePreferences()` opened with `if (client == null || user == null) return;`, and Private mode never has a Supabase session, so the page always returned on its first line and hydrated every field from this Mac's own SharedPreferences. That is the confirmed cause of the user's symptom: accent orange on the iPhone, yellow on the Mac, and a different app language. Fixed end to end — read, apply, re-read after a pull, and write back through the shared store.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **The read-back**: `_loadProfilePreferences` gains a Private-mode branch ahead of the Supabase one, reading via the new `DesktopPrivateDb.loadSettingsRow()` (a `SyncedSettingsStore.readAll` over the encrypted DB — row-first, legacy `profiles` column as fallback). Exposed to the app as `desktopSyncedSettingsProvider` (new `desktop/lib/features/settings/application/desktop_synced_settings.dart`), which returns `{}` in Supabase mode and swallows a locked/absent DB so the app root can listen to it safely.
    - **Applying, not just reading**: `applyDesktopSyncedSettings` pushes `theme_mode`/`accent_color` into `desktopAppearanceControllerProvider.applyProfile` and `language` into a new `DesktopLocaleController.applyProfile` (so the real locale changes, not just the field). `_applySyncedSettings` then updates the page fields, writes the local prefs mirror, and re-runs `_syncNotifications()` so pulled brief times/toggles actually reschedule. Keys the store has no value for are left untouched — the store omits "never set" keys precisely so a caller keeps its own default.
    - **App-wide, not page-local**: `EvolveDesktopApp` listens to `desktopSyncedSettingsProvider` and applies, so an iPhone theme/accent/language change repaints the Mac even with Settings closed. Deliberately NOT `fireImmediately` (that would mutate a provider mid-build).
    - **Post-pull re-hydration**: `refreshPrivateAfterPull` now also invalidates `desktopSyncedSettingsProvider`, matching mobile's `invalidatePrivateDataProviders` (which invalidates its `settingsProvider`). A setting changed on the iPhone lands on the Mac on the next pull instead of the next restart.
    - **Writes routed through the shared store**: `_syncProfile`'s private branch filters to `PrivateDbSchema.syncedSettingKeys`, encodes via `SyncedSettingsStore.encodeBool/encodeInt`, and calls the new `DesktopPrivateDb.writeSyncedSettings()` (dual-writes the per-key `user_settings` row AND the legacy column). `DesktopPrivateDb.sanitizeSettings` **no longer appends `{is_pro: 1, sentry_consent: 0}`** to every settings write — both are `deviceLocalProfileColumns` now, and stamping them meant toggling the Mac's 24h clock silently reset crash-report consent on the iPhone.
    - **One parser, shared with mobile**: desktop's `themeMode == 'light' ? light : dark` is replaced by `SettingsCodec.resolveIsDark` (new `DesktopAppearanceController.themeModeFor`), and `_languageCode` / `DesktopLocaleController._normalize` by `SettingsCodec.normalizeLanguage`. `DesktopAppearanceController.applyProfile` **no longer calls `_persist()`** — a read path must not write; it used to coerce `'system'` to `'dark'`, store it, and push the coercion to every device. A null argument now means "no opinion" and keeps the current value. The prefs mirror is written by the hydration paths that own the change.
    - **Defaults**: the brief-time field initialisers were `08:00`/`20:30` and won whenever prefs were empty (initState early-returns before the fallback lines), dragging the iPhone's briefs 60 and 30 minutes earlier. Now `SettingsCodec.defaultMorningBriefTime`/`defaultEveningReviewTime` (`09:00`/`21:00`), with `normalizeTimeOfDay` guarding malformed values. The prefs key and the DB column keep their different names on purpose (`notif_morning_brief_time` vs `morning_brief_time`).
    - **Reset to defaults**: writes only `syncedSettingKeys` through `_syncProfile`. `biometric_lock` is dropped from the payload (a reset on the Mac must not unlock the user's iPhone) and reset locally instead; the accent hex is derived from the accent actually applied rather than a second hardcoded literal.
    - **Tests**: new `desktop/test/settings_synced_readback_test.dart` (10 tests) — row-wins-over-stale-column read-back, no `is_pro`/`sentry_consent` stamping, a device-local key rejected by the store, theme/accent/locale actually applied, absent keys left alone, `applyProfile` not re-persisting its coercion, legacy `'italiano'` resolving identically on both apps, `refreshPrivateAfterPull` re-reading the settings, the provider staying empty outside Private mode, canonical 09:00/21:00. Verified: desktop `flutter analyze lib/ test/` clean (2 pre-existing warnings) and **461/461** tests passing.
  - *Current Status*: macOS side complete. `packages/evolve_sync/**` and `mobile/**` untouched by this change.

- [2026-07-21 12:00]: Mobile (iOS) — Focus Mode toggle, settings routed through SyncedSettingsStore, clobber fix, SettingsCodec adoption
  - *Details*: The iPhone side of the shared-settings work. Four defects, all of them things the Mac could do to the phone with no way for the phone to answer back. (1) macOS has a Focus Mode switch and the value SYNCS, but iOS had no UI for it anywhere — the only other mention was a key in the privacy-settings export JSON — while `_runNotificationSync` opens with `cancelAll()` and returns early whenever the flag is on. So the Mac could permanently silence the iPhone with no visible cause and no way to undo it on the phone. (2) `_saveToPrivate()` wrote a ~20-key map covering EVERY settings column on ANY single toggle, and `build()` seeds state synchronously from `_defaultSettings()` while `_loadPrivateSettings()` runs async — so a change made before that load resolved wrote DEFAULTS across every column, silently reverting settings only macOS has UI for. (3) `main.dart` applies SharedPreferences `pref_language` before the first frame, but Private mode never wrote that key, so a private cold start rendered in a stale cloud-era language until the load resolved. (4) `_ensureSafeAccentColor` rewrote the accent on THEME CHANGE and persisted the result, pushing a new colour to every device — and each mode's fallback is unreadable in the other, so flipping back pushed a third.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **Focus Mode UI**: new switch at the TOP of `notification_settings_screen.dart`, above the toggles it overrides, because a person whose reminders stopped opens that screen and the explanation must not be buried under the switches it is suppressing. i18n in all 5 locales (`notifications.focusHeader/focusMode/focusModeSubtitle/focusModeSyncNote`), title and detail copied verbatim from macOS so the same switch reads identically on both. The subtitle states that it syncs — cross-device silence has to be explainable.
    - **Settings routed through the shared store**: new `PrivateDataStore.loadSyncedSettings()` / `writeSyncedSettings()`, implemented in `private_local_database.dart` as `SyncedSettingsStore(db).readAll/writeAll(owner)`. The provider's `_syncedSettingsMap` / `_applySyncedSettings` are the only translation between `AppSettings` and `PrivateDbSchema.syncedSettingKeys`, and are exact inverses. `_privateBaseSettings` keeps the DEVICE-LOCAL half (biometric lock from `profiles`, verification prefs + stats filter from SharedPreferences); synced values layer on top. `updateSettingsRow` is now only reached for `biometric_lock` (and only when it actually moved) plus the AI-consent path — it re-stamps `is_pro`/`sentry_consent` and bumps `updated_at`, so calling it on unrelated toggles was pure sync noise. **Cloud (Supabase) mode is unchanged.**
    - **The clobber fix is DIFF-BASED per-key writes, not a load gate.** A write now carries only the keys that actually moved, whatever else the in-memory snapshot holds. Gating was rejected: it drops or defers a tap the user already made, and it does nothing about the second source of the same damage — a write issued long AFTER the load that still re-stamps every unrelated column and pushes it. The load additionally re-applies any edit made while it was in flight (`_preloadEdits`), so a toggle tapped during a cold start is not snapped back a few frames later. `_privateLoaded` is set in a `finally` so a failed load cannot wedge every later write in pre-load mode.
    - **iOS writes a deliberate SUBSET of `syncedSettingKeys`**: `pref_glass_effects`, `pref_start_week_on_monday`, `pref_show_weekend` and `tutorial_completed` have no iOS field, so emitting an invented default for them would push macOS's real value away. Pinned by test.
    - **SettingsCodec everywhere**: `AppLanguagePreference.normalize` now delegates to `SettingsCodec.normalizeLanguage` (public API kept — `localeOverrideFor`, `main.dart` and several screens call it). `main.dart`'s `themeMode == 'dark' ? dark : light` — which rendered LIGHT for the `'system'` the schema permits and macOS writes, while macOS rendered the same string DARK — is now `SettingsCodec.resolveIsDark`. Same for the dark-mode switch's own value and the custom-picker's too-dark warning in `app_settings_screen.dart`. Brief times go through `normalizeTimeOfDay` with `defaultMorningBriefTime`/`defaultEveningReviewTime`; the accent seed is now `SettingsCodec.defaultAccentColor` (`#FFFFFF`, was `#FAFAFA` on the Dart side against the DB's `#FFFFFF`).
    - **Accent hazard**: `_ensureSafeAccentColor` no longer runs on theme change at all. Readability is settled at PAINT time by the new `AppSettingsNotifier.readableAccent(color, themeMode, platformIsDark:)`, used by `main.dart`'s theme seeds and the swatch grid; the stored value stays whatever the user picked and nothing is pushed. The test itself is now a real WCAG contrast ratio (< 1.5, i.e. white-on-white) rather than a raw luminance threshold, and resolves `'system'` through the codec. The clamp survives only in `setAccentColor` — an explicit pick, on this device, in this moment, is the one point where correcting an invisible colour is honest.
    - **Cold-start language**: `_loadPrivateSettings` and the private write path mirror the resolved language into SharedPreferences `pref_language`. One-way cache; the private DB stays the source of truth and nothing reads it back in private mode. It is the only `pref_*` key the private path writes beyond the pre-existing biometric-lock mirror, and `settings_separation_test` now asserts exactly that.
    - **Tests**: new `test/focus_mode_toggle_test.dart` (6), `test/settings_clobber_test.dart` (7), `test/settings_codec_test.dart` (8); `settings_separation_test.dart` updated to the new contract plus a `pref_language` case. `FakePrivateDataStore` gains `syncedSettings`/`syncedWrites`/`settingsRowWrites`. Verified: `flutter analyze lib/` clean apart from the 4 pre-existing `macro_goals_screen.dart` issues, and **431/431** tests passing (was 409).
  - *Current Status*: iOS side complete. `packages/evolve_sync/**` and `desktop/**` untouched by this change. **Immediate next step**: on-device QA of the two-device settings round-trip (see TO_SIMO_DO.md).

- [2026-07-21 11:30]: Settings sync — post-review corrections (hardening commit 6/7)
  - *Details*: Both apps were wired onto the shared `SyncedSettingsStore`/`SettingsCodec` by parallel agents, each followed by an adversarial reviewer. Both reviewers returned **needs-work despite green tests**, and one finding mattered a great deal: the user's actual reported symptom was NOT fixed.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **The orange-vs-yellow root cause survived the first pass.** `DesktopAppearanceController.applyProfile` still ran `_ensureVisibleAccent` over the value pulled from the synced store, so each device coerced the SHARED accent against its OWN theme and the same stored value rendered differently — exactly the reported bug. Fixed by adopting the model mobile already uses: store the accent VERBATIM (in `build`, `setAccentColor`, `setThemeMode` and `applyProfile`), and substitute for legibility at PAINT time via the new `DesktopAppearanceController.readableAccent(color, brightness)`, applied in `evolve_desktop_app.dart` where `EvolveTheme.light/dark` are constructed. `setThemeMode` no longer rewrites the accent either — coercing on a theme flip persisted a new colour and pushed it to every device, so switching to dark mode on the Mac silently changed the iPhone's accent.

Adding `_persist()` there cross-contaminated the full-suite run (3 failures that passed in isolation, confirmed by a disable-and-rerun experiment). Re-assessed: `build()` reads the mirror and desktop's launch sync re-applies the synced value, so the real symptom is a frame or two of the old appearance, self-healing every session — not a permanent revert. A pure apply that flashes briefly beats a read path with side effects, which is the exact shape of the bug `applyProfile` already had once. Documented in the code.
    - **Test retargeted, not deleted**: `desktop_appearance_controller_test.dart`'s "theme changes keep the accent visible" asserted the coercion that was removed. Replaced with two tests pinning the NEW contract — a theme flip does not rewrite the stored accent (in either direction, no third colour invented), and legibility is applied at paint time without touching what is stored.
    - Verified: evolve_sync **153/153**, mobile **431/431**, desktop **462/462**; `flutter analyze lib/` reports zero errors on both apps.
  - *Known gaps carried forward (reviewer findings not yet addressed)*: mobile's `pref_language` mirror writes a private-mode value into the cloud settings store; a Focus Mode ON arriving from macOS renders the switch but does not cancel already-scheduled iOS reminders until something re-runs scheduling (the DANGEROUS direction — being unable to undo it — is fixed by the new toggle); desktop's private-mode read-back has no direct test; desktop collapses a `'system'` theme when writing its prefs mirror; the settings page applies synced settings twice on open; desktop never calls `SettingsCodec.normalizeAccentColor`; `biometric_lock` remains in `_settingsColumns` so a backup import can set it (it is in `localOnlyColumns`, so it still cannot cross the sync boundary).
  - *Current Status*: Commits 1-6 complete. **Immediate next step**: on-device verification of the full settings round-trip (see TO_SIMO_DO.md), iPhone first per the agreed migration order.

- [2026-07-21 12:10]: iCloud Sync — cadence parity (hardening commit 7/7)
  - *Details*: On-device testing confirmed the full settings round-trip works in both directions (iOS log shows `{pushed: 2, applied: 0}` for a two-key settings change and `{pushed: 0, applied: 3}` for three arriving from macOS — per-key records behaving exactly as designed, with no whole-row clobber). The user reported having to force-quit the Mac app to see a change. Diagnosis: NOT a defect — the desktop periodic poll was 15 minutes, and window-refocus only fires when you actually switch away and back, so a focused window could wait a quarter of an hour. Cmd+Q hit the launch-sync path, which is why a restart looked instant.
    Also confirmed from the log export: every `[ERROR]` line is stamped 2026-07-20 22:26, i.e. the pre-reset key-split records already diagnosed. No new errors. The recurring `[WARN] Screen Time not authorized` is the auto-verified-habits feature, unrelated to sync.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - `desktop_sync_lifecycle.dart`: `_periodicInterval` 15 min → **3 min**. Without CloudKit push this timer is the only way a focused Mac learns about an iPhone edit; 15 minutes reads as "sync is broken" long before it reads as "sync is slow". Each poll is a delta fetch that returns nothing when idle.
    - `mobile/lib/main.dart`: iOS gained the two triggers it never had — a **launch sync** (`addPostFrameCallback`; a cold start does not emit `resumed`, so the first pull of a session previously waited for the user to background the app and return) and a **3-minute periodic timer**, both routed through a new mode-gated `_syncIfPrivate()` that guards on `mounted`. Timer cancelled in `dispose`. iOS previously had ONLY `resumed` + the write debounce, so an iPhone left open never learned about a Mac edit at all.
    - Both apps now poll on the same interval, so the two clients behave identically.
    - Verified: mobile **431/431**, desktop **462/462**, evolve_sync **153/153**; zero analyze errors on both apps.
  - *Deferred by explicit decision*: CloudKit **push subscriptions** (`CKSubscription` + silent push). That is the correct fix — the polling exists only because push was deferred, and the code says so. It needs native Swift on both platforms plus a push entitlement, and cannot be exercised without real hardware, so it was kept out of a cadence tweak to land on its own.

- [2026-07-21 14:20]: iCloud Sync — CloudKit push subscriptions (commit 8/8)
  - *Details*: Replaced the "wait up to 3 minutes" latency with a `CKDatabaseSubscription` silent push, so a change on one device reaches the other in seconds. Implemented after the user enabled the Push Notifications capability on App ID `com.simo.evolve`.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **Dart contract**: `CloudKitBridge.ensureSubscription()` (documented as idempotent and non-fatal), implemented in `MethodChannelCloudKitBridge` (swallows `MissingPluginException`/`PlatformException`), the fake, and the two test wrapper bridges. `MethodChannelCloudKitBridge.setRemoteChangeHandler/clearRemoteChangeHandler` receive the native `remoteChange` invocation.
    - **Service**: `_ensureSubscribed()` guarded by a PROCESS-scoped `_subscribed` flag — once per process, not once ever, so a device that failed (offline/transient) retries on a later sync instead of being written off until the next launch. Called from `_enable()` (so a user who just enabled sync gets pushes immediately) and `_syncNow()`. Failures are logged, never rethrown.
    - **Native (both apps)**: `CKDatabaseSubscription` with a FIXED subscription id (`evolve-private-zone-changes`) so re-registering is idempotent — CloudKit subscriptions outlive reinstalls, and a fresh uuid per launch would pile up duplicates and deliver one push per duplicate. `shouldSendContentAvailable = true`, no alert/badge/sound. `.serverRejectedRequest` (already registered) is treated as SUCCESS. The `evolve/cloudkit` channel is now RETAINED in a static (it was a local `let`, i.e. one-directional) so `notifyRemoteChange()` can invoke back into Dart.
    - **Push handlers**: iOS `didReceiveRemoteNotification:fetchCompletionHandler:` and macOS `didReceiveRemoteNotification:`, both filtering on `CKNotification.containerIdentifier` and forwarding anything else to `super`. iOS returns `.newData` deliberately — reporting `.noData` teaches iOS the app's pushes are not worth waking for and it throttles them harder.
    - **Entitlements/plist**: `aps-environment = development` in `mobile/ios/Runner/Runner.entitlements` and both macOS entitlements files (Xcode rewrites to `production` on distribution export); `UIBackgroundModes = [remote-notification]` in the iOS Info.plist. All four validated with `plutil -lint`. `registerForRemoteNotifications()` on both platforms — silent pushes need NO user permission, so this adds no prompt.
    - **App wiring**: the push handler routes into the SAME mode-gated sync as every other trigger (`_syncIfPrivate` on mobile, `_sync` on desktop). Push changes only WHEN sync runs, never what it does — no second sync path to drift from the hardened one. Handlers cleared in `dispose`.
    - **The 3-minute poll is deliberately KEPT.** Apple does not guarantee silent-push delivery. Push is a latency optimisation layered over polling; that is also what makes shipping untested native code acceptable, since the failure mode is exactly the pre-push behaviour.
    - **Tests**: new `test/push_subscription_test.dart` (4) — registered once not per sync, a device that cannot subscribe still syncs and still uploads, a failed registration retries on a later sync, and the poll alone still converges with no push ever delivered. Verified: evolve_sync **157/157**, mobile **431/431**, desktop **462/462**.
  - *Current Status*: All 8 commits complete. **Immediate next step**: refresh provisioning profiles, rebuild BOTH apps, and run the on-device checklist in TO_SIMO_DO.md. No native push code has ever been executed.

- [2026-07-21 15:05]: Desktop log persistence + sync trigger attribution (diagnostics)
  - *Details*: The user reported that a change made with BOTH apps open takes a long time to appear, while quitting and reopening syncs instantly. Their Mac log exports could not confirm or refute this: every one contained ~5 entries beginning at a fresh launch, while the iPhone's spanned 40+ minutes.
    Root cause of the blind spot: **`desktop/lib/core/app_logger.dart` never persisted anything** — no `loadLogs`, no file write, buffer process-local. Mobile has always persisted (`AppLogger.loadLogs()` at startup, debounced `writeAsString`). So the Mac's history was erased on every launch — and since quitting and reopening is precisely the workaround the user applies when sync looks stuck, **the action taken to work around the problem destroyed the evidence of it**. Every Mac log supplied so far begins AFTER the interesting moment.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - `LogEntry.toJson`/`fromJson` added on desktop (it had neither); `AppLogger.loadLogs()` reads `getApplicationSupportDirectory()/app_logs.json`, skipping malformed entries individually rather than discarding the whole history; `_scheduleSave()` debounced at 2s and fire-and-forget (logging must never fail the operation it observes); `clearLogs()` now clears disk too; `loadLogs()` called first thing in `desktop/lib/main.dart`.
    - `@visibleForTesting AppLogger.persistenceEnabled` (default TRUE, opt-OUT) — the 2s timer fails any widget test that ends with it pending. Opt-out rather than opt-in deliberately: a logger that silently stops recording because a flag was not set is the exact failure this change exists to prevent. `desktop/test/app_logs_test.dart` disables it in setUp and restores in tearDown.
    - **Sync trigger attribution**: `PrivateSyncService.syncNow({String reason})` threaded through both apps, logging `Sync starting (trigger: push|poll|launch|resume|write|manual)`. Previously every trigger logged an identical "Sync starting...", so a working push was indistinguishable from the poll that would have run anyway — making the one thing worth verifying on device impossible to verify. Six test fakes updated for the widened signature.
    - Verified: evolve_sync **157/157**, mobile **431/431**, desktop **462/462**; zero analyze errors.
  - *Current Status*: Push subscriptions register successfully on BOTH devices (`Zone change subscription registered` in both logs, no errors). Whether they DELIVER is still unproven — no `trigger: push` has appeared in any log, but no supplied log covers a window where both devices were awake and a change was made on one. The instrumentation to settle it now exists on both platforms.

- [2026-07-21 16:30]: CloudKit push — diagnosed, deprioritised; polls shortened to 60s
  - *Details*: Definitive on-device test. macOS 16:11:33 `trigger: write, pushed: 1` (a new monthly goal); iPhone 16:12:02 `trigger: poll, applied: 1` — delivered 29s later BY THE POLL. **No `trigger: push` has ever appeared on either device**, including on the iPhone, which holds a valid APNs token (`Registered ... token 32 bytes`). So push is not delivered even to a device that can receive it.
    Established along the way, each by adding a diagnostic rather than guessing:
    1. `applicationDidFinishLaunching` is **never invoked** in the macOS app — the Mac was never even requesting a push token. Registration moved into `CloudKitSyncBridge.register()`, on the path that provably runs (the channel and the subscription both initialise there).
    2. With registration actually happening, macOS now reports `[APNs] Registration FAILED: OSStatus error 13` — a signing/provisioning problem (the running binary is not signed with a profile carrying `aps-environment`), outside the code.
    3. iOS registers successfully on every launch.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **Fixed a false success I introduced**: `ensureSubscription` treated `CKError.serverRejectedRequest` as "already registered" and returned success. That is a genuine rejection, not an idempotency signal — and re-registering an existing subscription id is not an error in CloudKit anyway (`CKModifySubscriptionsOperation` updates in place), so there was no case to special-case. The app may therefore have been logging "Zone change subscription registered" on both devices while no subscription existed. Now every failure is reported, with the CKError code, through `logNative`. This was the same report-success-on-failure pattern the whole hardening pass exists to remove.
    - **Polls shortened 3 min → 60 s** on both apps (`desktop_sync_lifecycle.dart`, `mobile/lib/main.dart`). Sized for reality rather than design intent: since push has never been observed to deliver, the timer IS the sync path, not a backstop. Foreground-only on iOS, so it costs nothing while the app is off screen.
    - Push code left wired: it costs nothing while silent, and the subscription-error logging may yet reveal why CloudKit is not sending.
    - Verified: evolve_sync **157/157**, mobile **431/431**, desktop **462/462**; zero analyze errors.
  - *Current Status*: Sync is correct and converges within ~60s on both devices. Push is registered but unconfirmed; macOS additionally cannot receive (OSStatus 13, signing). **Deliberately deprioritised** — the remaining value is latency, not correctness, and the outstanding items in earlier entries (Screen Time / freezes DB / AI-config coverage, desktop settings read-back tests) matter more.

- [2026-07-21 18:40]: iCloud sync Tier A — the system stops reporting success while failing
  - *Details*: Closed the three Tier A correctness gaps from `ICLOUD_SYNC_STATE.md`, plus two defects of the same family found while verifying them. Every claim below was proved by running a test that FAILED first — none is asserted from reading.
    **A1 — `last_full_sync_at` was stamped unconditionally.** Confirmed empirically: a push in which the only record was rejected reported `pushed=0`, left the record dirty, and still stamped the timestamp with the current time. Both apps render that verbatim as "Last synced 14:42". Worse, the status headline in BOTH apps returned "Up to date" as its unconditional fallback — `SyncDiagnostics.isFullySynced` was documented as "the ONLY condition under which a UI may claim up to date" and neither status line consulted it. (A first-pass reviewer argued A1 was already handled because the *details row* is gated; a widget test showed "Up to date" rendering over 5000 stranded rows, which is why the claim was tested rather than believed.)
    **A1b — pull failures were silently discarded.** `markError` is a bare `UPDATE ... WHERE record_name = ?`. For a record arriving from another device that this build has never seen there IS no `sync_state` row — `stateOf()` returned null moments earlier — so the update matched zero rows and wrote nothing. Meanwhile `_pull` held the change token for it. Net effect, proved in a test: `isFullySynced == true`, a clean diagnostics report, and a device that re-downloads the entire delta on every sync forever without converging.
    **C1 — un-parking destroyed unpushed local edits.** The listed C1 (quarantineRecord's `ON CONFLICT`) is NOT a defect; that clause is specified verbatim in its own doc comment and forcing `dirty = 0` there would drop pending writes out of the push queue. The real bug is its counterpart: `clearUndecryptableParks`/`clearUnknownTableParks` rewound `updated_at` to the epoch for every matching row with no `dirty` filter. Proved: a habit with a pending local edit at `2020-01-01T10:00` came back as `1970-01-01T00:00` while still queued for push. That loses the edit twice — on pull, any remote copy (even an OLDER one) then beats `localMs = 0` and overwrites it; on push, a pending delete goes out as a tombstone stamped 1970 and loses LWW on every other device, so the deletion silently never propagates while the sync reports success.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - `SyncResult` gains `pushFailed`, `pushConflicted`, `pullIncomplete` and `bool get fullySynced`; `syncNow` stamps `last_full_sync_at` only when `fullySynced`, and logs the counts when it does not. `_push`/`_pull` now return `_PushOutcome`/`_PullOutcome` instead of a bare int and a positional tuple. `enable()` forwards the new fields — it runs the first push of the user's whole history, the most likely one to be throttled.
    - `SyncLocalStore.markPullError` (upserting, mirroring `quarantineRecord`) replaces `markError` at the two engine PULL sites. `markError` stays for the PUSH path, where the row provably exists because it came from `dirtyEntries()`.
    - `SyncDiagnostics` gains `heldByReason` + `totalHeld`, and `totalStuck` (= errors + parked + held). Held records are counted apart from parked ones because the advice is opposite: a held record is re-delivered by the very next sync, a parked one only by a full re-fetch. Both apps now call `totalStuck` instead of hand-summing two buckets — they had hand-summed, and both would have silently under-counted the moment a bucket was added.
    - Both apps' status headline now returns a new `icloudSync.statusNotSynced` string (added in all five locales, `dart run slang` re-run) whenever `!isFullySynced`, instead of "Up to date".
    - `clearUndecryptableParks`/`clearUnknownTableParks` rewritten as `rawUpdate` with `updated_at = CASE WHEN dirty = 1 THEN updated_at ELSE ? END`. The rewind exists so a re-delivered record beats a parked PLACEHOLDER, and a placeholder already sits at `quarantineStamp` — so the only rows the rewind actually changed were the dirty ones it corrupted. The row count still counts every matched row, because the engine keys its full re-fetch off it.
    - `PrivateSyncStatus.ownerPending` added. Desktop's `private_mode_recovery` inferred "enable deferred" from `lastSyncedAt == null`; once A1 stopped stamping an incomplete sync, a rate-limited first pull would have been reported as "waiting for iCloud Keychain" — a wait with nothing at the end of it. It now asks the engine instead of guessing. (Mobile used `isEnabled` and was unaffected.)
    - **A2/A3, native — `retryDelay`/`scheduleRetry` added to BOTH Swift bridges.** Handles `.requestRateLimited`, `.serviceUnavailable` and `.zoneBusy`, honouring the server's `CKError.retryAfterSeconds` when present and falling back to 2s/4s/8s, capped at 3 retries. Applied to every operation. `qualityOfService` went from 1 operation per bridge to all 7 — `.utility` everywhere except `zoneHasRecords`, which is `.userInitiated` because it blocks the enable the user is watching and decides whether a second E2E key gets minted. `.partialFailure` is deliberately NOT retried: its per-record blocks already ran and the engine retries exactly the failed records next sync.
    - Both `AppDelegate.swift` files were patched from a single script, and every line changed is byte-identical between them (verified by diff — the only difference in the whole CloudKit enum is the macOS APNs registration block).
    - **Native verification status**: no Xcode on the dev machine, so nothing was built or run. Both bridges DO typecheck against the real CloudKit framework (`swiftc -typecheck`, Swift 6.3, macOS SDK), which confirms the API surface but no runtime behaviour. On-device checklist in `TO_SIMO_DO.md`.
    - New tests: `last_full_sync_honesty_test.dart` (7), `pull_failure_visibility_test.dart` (5), `unpark_preserves_pending_edit_test.dart` (4), plus 4 status-line widget tests per app and a desktop recovery test. `FakeCloudKitBridge` gains `failSaveFor`/`failSaveCode` so a push failure can be exercised at all — previously no test could reach that branch, which is how the unconditional stamp survived.
    - Verified: evolve_sync **173/173**, mobile **435/435** + 0 analyze errors, desktop **467/467** + 0 analyze errors (with the required dart-defines).
  - *Current Status*: Tier A closed in Dart and written-but-unrun in Swift. Tier C (C2–C17) in progress. Tier B and Tier D untouched by design — Tier B needs hardware, Tier D is the owner's decision.

- [2026-07-21 20:15]: iCloud sync Tier C — C2–C17 closed, plus one shared-package hole
  - *Details*: Every item was verified against the code before being fixed, by two independent passes (an investigator and an adversarial refuter). That was not ceremony: **three first-pass verdicts were wrong in the dangerous direction**, and one "NOT_A_DEFECT" was concealing a live data-loss bug (see the Tier A entry, C1). The list in `ICLOUD_SYNC_STATE.md` was described as "a starting point, not ground truth", and it earned that description — C1 as written is not a defect, C5/C7/C12/C13 were misdescribed, and C2's stated consequence was understated.
    **C2** (`_ms` sentinel mismatch) — real, and worse than described. The engine's `0` and the store's `-1` meet in exactly one comparison, `_remoteWinsNaturalKey`. Two equally-unorderable stamps compared UNEQUAL, so the deterministic id tiebreak that exists to make both devices pick the same survivor never ran. Both devices therefore concluded "remote wins", each deleted its own row, and the tombstones crossed: **the row was annihilated on both machines**, not merely mis-resolved. Aligned on `0` (a named `unorderableMs`). Note the prescribed fix from both passes — align on `-1` — was rejected and would have caused data loss: a garbage-stamped row would then tie with the "no local record" sentinel and be silently stranded on every device that had never seen it.
    **C3** (future-skew guard) — real. A record stamped beyond `now + 5min` rewound the change token every sync, forever, re-downloading and re-discarding the whole delta. Split into two tiers: skew inside 24h still DEFERS and holds the token (self-limiting — the band slides forward and `now` reaches it within a day), beyond 24h the record is PARKED and the token advances, with the park EXPIRING when the clock passes its stamp. The record is neither re-fetched forever nor lost, which the regression test drives with an injected clock.
    **C4–C8 (mobile)**, **C9–C17 (desktop)** — all fixed with named regression tests. C12 was fixed by NARROWING (`biometric_lock` removed from the desktop allow-list), never by widening. C15/C16 are the accent bug a previous session "fixed" while the user-visible bug survived; the new `accent_parity_test.dart` was mutation-tested here — reverting `defaultAccent` to `0xFFFAFAFA` kills 4 tests, and bypassing `normalizeAccentColor` kills 4 more.
    **New finding, not on the list**: `SyncLocalStore.applyUpsert` filtered pulled payloads by `_columnsOf(table)` alone and never subtracted `localOnlyColumns`, so the schema's documented "stripped on push AND preserved on apply" was only half true. It held solely because every CURRENT sender strips — a property of the fleet, not of this device. A field build predating `deviceLocalProfileColumns` still pushes those columns, and a modern device wrote them: a pulled `is_pro = 1` is an **in-app-purchase bypass**, a pulled `sentry_consent` grants consent on a device that was never asked, and a pulled `avatar_url` points the UI at a file on another machine. Now stripped on apply as well. This is a narrowing of an existing list, not an addition to it; nothing on the wire changes and older builds are unaffected.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - `PrivateDbSchema.syncedSettingKeys` and `localOnlyColumns` were NOT widened — verified by diff; `private_db_schema.dart` is untouched.
    - `SyncEngine` gains an injectable `clock` (production never passes it): the skew guard's correctness argument is entirely about the passage of time, and none of it is testable against a clock that only reads "now".
    - Desktop's appearance control changed shape (binary dark-mode switch → three-option select) so `'system'` is expressible; the orphaned `darkMode`/`darkModeDetail` keys were removed from all five locales. **Visible UI change — wants on-device QA.**
    - Verified: evolve_sync **183/183** (was 157 at session start), mobile **449/449** (was 431), desktop **491/491** (was 462). Zero analyze errors in both apps; the only warnings are the pre-existing ones named in the handoff.
    - Full-diff audit for the recurring failure signature (swallowed errors, defaults on failure, error codes treated as success) returns nothing in either the Dart or the Swift diff. The one Dart `?? 0` is a `COUNT(*)` coalesce matching the surrounding idiom; every Swift `return nil` means "not retryable", so the error propagates and IS reported.
  - *Current Status*: Tier A and Tier C are closed in Dart. The Swift retry/QoS work compiles and typechecks but has **never been executed** — see `TO_SIMO_DO.md`. Tier B (hardware verification) and Tier D (product/legal decisions) remain untouched by design. Residual items that need their own change, chiefly the C2 root cause in both apps' backup import, are listed in `TO_SIMO_DO.md`.

- [2026-07-21 22:10]: Avatar — a missing local file was deleting the picture on every device
  - *Details*: Reported as "the profile icon is a black circle on iPhone, but the Mac shows it perfectly". Two separate defects, and the second is destructive.
    **The black circle is the FALLBACK.** `assets/images/default_avatar.png` is a black image with white line-art, so `ProfileAvatarImage`'s `errorBuilder` firing is visually indistinguishable from a rendering failure. Nothing was rendering wrong — the app was reporting that it could not load the file, in a form that reads as a glitch.
    **Root cause: `profiles.avatar_url` stores an ABSOLUTE path** rooted at the app container (`getApplicationSupportDirectory()`), written by both the picker and the sync-pull. iOS regenerates that container's UUID across reinstalls, so the stored string goes stale while the image itself never moves. macOS paths are stable in practice — which is exactly the asymmetry the user described.
    **The destructive half.** `readAvatarBytes()` returned null for two unrelated situations — "the user removed their avatar" and "the file could not be read" — and `_encodeAvatar` replicated BOTH as a tombstone. `markAllDirty()` (re-enable / re-key) re-queues the avatar on nothing more than `avatar_url` being non-empty, never checking the file exists. So: reinstall → stale path → re-enable → tombstone → **the good copy is deleted from the zone and from every other device, irrecoverably**, while the sync reports success. Proved with a test before fixing: `avatar record in cloud is now deleted=true`.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - `SyncAvatarStore.hasAvatarConfigured()` added (deliberately abstract, not defaulted — a default of `false` would silently preserve the destructive behaviour for any implementation that forgot to override it, which is the precise failure mode being removed; a compile error is the correct forcing function). `_encodeAvatar` now tombstones only when NO avatar is configured.
    - A configured-but-unreadable avatar is a push FAILURE: counted into `SyncResult.pushFailed`, so `last_full_sync_at` is not stamped and both apps report the device as not fully synced; `sync_state.last_error` carries `SyncLocalStore.avatarFileMissingReason`, which names the recovery ("the image is still in iCloud — re-select it to republish"). Nothing is pushed, so the zone keeps the good copy.
    - `avatarPathCandidates()` added to the shared package — pure, dart:io-free, and called by BOTH apps so they cannot drift. Resolves the stored value's BASENAME against the current container first, falling back to the literal path. Self-healing rather than a migration: no schema change, idempotent, and it repairs databases already carrying a stale path on the next read. Handles both path separators (a restored backup can carry a foreign one).
    - Both apps' UI now renders the RESOLVED path. Mobile threads it through `_fromPrivateRow(row, avatarPath:)` rather than `copyWith` — `copyWith`'s `??` would have kept the stale stored path in exactly the case that must fall back to the default avatar. `PrivateDataStore.resolveAvatarPath()` added to the interface (and the test fake) so the provider is not coupled to the concrete class.
    - New tests: `avatar_missing_file_test.dart` (4 — lost file does not tombstone; it IS reported; a genuine removal still tombstones; a healthy avatar still uploads) and `avatar_path_test.dart` (8). All four in the first file were watched failing before the fix.
    - Verified: evolve_sync **195/195**, mobile **449/449**, desktop **491/491**; zero analyze errors, no new warnings.
  - *Current Status*: Fixed. **The user's existing cloud copy may already be a tombstone** from before this fix — re-selecting the image once republishes it. Note the desktop suite has two network-dependent tests that flake intermittently (`SocketException: Network is unreachable`); three consecutive full runs passed 491/491.

New `_EmptyState` case (`t.stats.noActiveHabits`, `LucideIcons.listFilter`) distinguishes "filtered to zero" from the pre-existing "truly zero habits" empty state (`t.stats.createHabitForAnalysis`), so a user with only ended habits isn't told to go create one.

    - Verified: `flutter analyze` — 0 new issues (4 pre-existing, unrelated). `flutter test` with the dummy Supabase dart-defines — 491/491 passing.
  - *Current Status*: Implemented and verified headless (analyze + test only — no Xcode on this machine). **Needs on-device QA on the Mac mini**: visually confirm the new segmented control's layout/spacing at both the wide and <640px breakpoints, and confirm the persisted choice survives an app restart.

- [2026-07-22 10:30]: AI Coach starter prompts — generic → data-personalized (desktop + mobile)
  - *Details*: Replaced the 19 generic, motivational one-liner starter chips ("🔥 Give me a boost to get started!", "🧠 How can I avoid distractions?") — which produced generic pep-talk answers because the *prompts* were generic — with a catalogue of **data-personalized** prompts that inject the user's real numbers and demand an actionable answer. Went through a full `/grill-me` pass with Simone: the chosen prompt "jobs" are **Diagnose & fix**, **Weekly review**, and **Build & connect** (the "prioritize now" job was explicitly dropped as too close to the old pep-talk); empty/new-user and all-caught-up states get context-appropriate build/level-up prompts instead of filler. The 10-prompt set: `diagnoseWeakestHabit`, `goalOnTrack`, `weeklyReviewDown`/`Up`, `protectStreak`, `alignHabitsToGoal`, `designHabitForGoal`, `raiseTheBar`, `firstStep`, `whatCanYouHelp`.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **Label/payload decoupling.** Each prompt is now a `CoachPrompt {label, payload}` — the chip shows a short emoji **label** (e.g. `🩺 Fix my weakest habit`), but the message *sent* is the engineered **payload** (`'Meditation' is my weakest habit this week — 2/5 days done. What's the most likely reason I keep missing it, and two specific fixes I can apply this week?`). This kept the chips scannable while letting the payload carry real data + a request for depth. It also let the payloads trip the coach persona's *existing* `"…max 3-4 sentences, unless the user explicitly asks for depth"` exception, so **no system-prompt change was needed** — free chat stays snappy, analytical chips opt into a structured reply. On mobile this removed a hack that stripped the leading emoji off the chip text before sending.
    - **Emoji only in the UI**, never in the payload sent to the model (per Simone).
    - **Pure, testable selector.** New `desktop/lib/features/ai_coach/domain/coach_prompts.dart` and `mobile/lib/core/coach_prompts.dart` (kept in lock-step) expose `CoachPrompt`, `CoachPromptFacts`, and `List<CoachPrompt> buildCoachPrompts(facts)`. Selection = a fixed **salience tier** (weakest habit → weekly momentum → best streak) followed by an **evergreen tail** (goal-on-track / align / design / raise-the-bar / first-step / discovery) rotated by message count so it stays fresh; de-duped by payload, capped at 4. Presentation computes the facts from live state and delegates — mirrors the old `buildAiSuggestions` contract.
    - **Facts wiring.** Desktop reads `DashboardSnapshot` (per-habit `weeklyProgress`/`streak`, `currentWeekCompletionRate`/`previousWeekCompletionRate`, goal `state`/`title`). Mobile has no precomputed streak or goal progress, so it derives the same facts locally: weakest habit + full-week momentum from `habitLogsProvider`, streak from `core/streak_utils.dart`'s `computeStreak`. Goal prompts use the **title only** (no due/pct) so the two clients stay at exact parity despite mobile's `MacroGoal` lacking a cheap progress/due-label. Share toggles (`shareHabits`/`shareGoals`) gate every fact so a hidden dimension never leaks into a chip.
    - **i18n.** Swapped `ai.suggestions` (19 flat keys) → `ai.coachPrompts` (10 × `{label, payload}`) in all 10 JSONs (desktop+mobile × en/it/es/de/ar), surgical regex replace (clean diffs), `dart run slang` re-run for both apps.

A final human native-speaker glance is nice-to-have but non-blocking.

- [2026-07-22 12:30]: iCloud sync `InvalidCipherTextException` — diagnosis + empty-zone key-split hardening (packages/evolve_sync + both apps, native)
  - *Details*: A TestFlight sync log showed `InvalidCipherTextException` on 8 records (`goals`/`goal_category_settings`/`profiles`) plus a `[SecureStorage] DEBUG build` line and a private-DB lockout→`[PrivateRecovery]` cascade. Went through a full `/grill-me` pass + a 5-agent read-only investigation workflow before touching code. **Root cause (settled): a dev/test-environment artifact, not a shipping-config bug.** The exception is an AES-256-GCM MAC failure = this device's E2E sync key ≠ the key those CloudKit records were sealed with (a "key split"). The failing binary was a **debug** build (`kDebugMode` const-folds the `[SecureStorage]` warning out of release), so the log is a local `flutter run`, not the TestFlight product; the lockout is from mixing debug + release builds on one Mac (SQLCipher key in a plaintext dev-file vs the Keychain). iCloud Keychain is on + one Apple ID + shared team prefix `8528AN28A3`, so prefix rotation is not the culprit. The one split cause that survives correct signing is the **empty-zone mint race**: two devices enabling on a still-empty zone before either's key propagates both pass the `zoneHasRecords` guard and both mint. The engine already *detects* splits (`undecryptableCount` → key-split card + `resetSyncFromThisDevice` on both apps) and *quarantines* undecryptable records (commit `f4e1431`, which the stale TestFlight binary predates), so the immediate fix is operational (rebuild from `main` + reset-from-iPhone). This change additionally **closes the race at the source**, plus closes a desktop test gap.
  - *Tech Notes*:
    - **New bridge primitive `CloudKitBridge.tryClaimFirstMint(ownerId) → Future<bool?>`** (`packages/evolve_sync/lib/src/cloudkit_bridge.dart`): server-side atomic arbitration the `zoneHasRecords` READ cannot provide (a read can't be atomic with the mint that follows). Concrete **default returns `null`** so no existing implementer breaks and the guard is **fail-open**: `true` = won (or idempotently already-ours), `false` = another device won ⇒ defer, `null` = no native support / inconclusive ⇒ fall through to the prior behaviour. New top-level const `kKeyOwnerSentinelRecordName = '__evolve_key_owner__'`.
    - **Engine guard** (`sync_engine.dart` `enable()`): when about to mint (`!force && readKey()==null`), the existing `zoneHasRecords` defer runs first (unchanged); if the zone is empty it now calls `tryClaimFirstMint(localOwner)` and defers with `keyPending` only on a definitive `false`. Strictly additive — it can only turn a would-be second mint into a defer, never the reverse. `_pull` skips the sentinel record so it is neither applied nor quarantined.
    - **Native (both `AppDelegate.swift`)**: new `tryClaimFirstMint`/`claimSentinel` — ensures the zone, then does an `.ifServerRecordUnchanged` create of the singleton `__evolve_key_owner__` record keyed on the owner id (atomic create-if-absent; `.serverRecordChanged` ⇒ read the existing `owner`, idempotent for the same device, else lose). `zoneHasRecords` now excludes the sentinel from its record/tombstone blocks so a device that claimed-then-crashed can still re-mint. Fails open (returns `nil`) on any inconclusive error. Registered in the method dispatch. **NOT compiled here (no Xcode) — see TO_SIMO_DO.md.**
    - **Test double** (`fake_cloudkit_bridge.dart`): models the atomic claim + a `supportsFirstMintClaim` flag for the fail-open path; materialises the sentinel as a real zone record (so the pull-skip is exercised) while `zoneHasRecords` excludes it.
    - **Tests**: 5 new in `sync_key_split_test.dart` (pure claim-guard defer, two-device convergence to one key, crash idempotency, fail-open mint, sentinel-not-quarantined); 2 new desktop widget tests in `icloud_sync_card_test.dart` covering the previously-untested key-split status label + `resetSyncFromThisDevice` recovery row. Forwarded the new method in the two `sync_convergence_test.dart` bridge wrappers.
    - Verified headless: `flutter analyze` (evolve_sync) clean; `flutter test` evolve_sync **200/200**, desktop `icloud_sync_card_test.dart` **13/13**.
  - *Current Status*: Dart + tests implemented and verified headless. **Native Swift is written but UNBUILT (no Xcode on this machine)** — must be compiled + on-device-verified on the Mac mini (both apps). Because the guard is fail-open, shipping the Dart without the native method is a no-op (identical to today); the race only closes once the native `tryClaimFirstMint` is built into both apps. Operational reconverge for the current split is in TO_SIMO_DO.md.
